# Pull 9 — Phase 3: Loom's layer on V2 in four tracks

Phase 3 builds everything that makes Loom's workstream layer work end to end on
the Phase 2 substrate, per `plans/upstream-pull9-phase3-tracks/plan.mdx`
(revision 4, amended in place by the Phase 3 orchestrator — see DL-302). Phase 2's
record is `docs/upstream-sync/30-cadence-pull-9-phase2.md` (DL-192–290); this
note continues the decision-log numbering from **DL-300**. Every call a coder,
reviewer or the orchestrator makes that the plan left open — or that departs from
it — is a row in §3. Phase 4 and Carl audit from here.

## 1. Upstream boundary

- Phase 2 pinned `upstream/main` at `3e6b45028c`. At the Phase 3 boundary
  `upstream/main` had advanced **39 commits to `6302d66f77`**. Merge commit
  **`0235b86c36`** (11 textual conflicts, resolved by the orchestrator keeping
  upstream's structure plus Loom's marked lines — DL-301). `UPSTREAM_BASE` =
  `6302d66f77`.
- What moved: Effect **4.0.1 stable** (`194c73f3f9` — every `effect/unstable/<x>`
  import is now `effect/<x>`); server service modules imported **as namespaces**
  (`6302d66f77`); upstream migrations **057/058**; a new **`thread.stop`**
  command as the human Stop path (`f32c23cf1a`); provider-native **`/goal`** for
  Codex and Claude (`a172e1c4e8`); the **`secret_request`** turn item and
  `request_secret` MCP tool (`9f61ba6741`); cheaper shell refreshes
  (`c138163c8c`); PR-watch rate-limit and wake fixes; `delegated-tasks.stop` on
  `run.interrupt holdQueue`; HTML pages inline (`677d1527c3`); webhook
  automations.
- Every line reference in the Phase 3 plan predates the boundary; anchor by
  symbol.

## 2. Environment and ranges

- Phase 2's §2 carries over verbatim: `CI=true vp i --no-frozen-lockfile`; server
  tests need git ≥ 2.56 (`PATH=/home/Carl/.cache/pull9-git256/bin:$PATH`);
  `--no-verify` on merge-touching commits; never `git stash`; kill only captured
  PIDs; smoke homes under `<worktree>/.t3/`; live DB copies only via
  `VACUUM INTO` from `~/.t3/cockpit/userdata/state.sqlite`; nothing writes to
  `~/.t3/cockpit`, `~/.t3/userdata`, `~/loom-releases`, the global pi or `~/.pi`.
- Migrations: Phase 3 uses **1049–1051 only** (1049 usage ledger, 1050 reroute,
  1051 free for a track that discovers a table); 1052 is Phase 4's.
- **DL ranges, assigned up front** (Phase 2's parallel chains collided — DL-244,
  DL-247): orchestrator and boundary **300–329**; track 3a **330–359**; track 3b
  **360–389**; track 3c **390–419**; track 3d **420–449**; integration reviewer
  and smoke **450–479**. A track that exhausts its range continues at 480+ and
  says so in its report.

| DL-332 | 3a-0 arm — `runtime-request.create` in `Orchestrator.loom.ts`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | P3-26 puts the request on the run's `rootNodeId`, but upstream's `runtime-request.respond` emits `node.updated { status: completed }` on `request.nodeId` — so any respond reaching upstream's code (3a-4's no-waiter fall-through) would complete the live run's ROOT node                                                                                                                                              | **amends P3-26's node choice:** the arm writes a dedicated request node, exactly as upstream's adapters do (`PiAdapterV2`, `CodexAdapterV2`). It refuses (receipted) a request id without the prefix, an empty `questions`, a missing thread, no blocking run (`preparing \| starting \| running \| waiting`, highest ordinal) or one whose `rootNodeId` is null, and any pending runtime request on the thread (upstream's native ones included). It emits on the commanded thread, in the Pi adapter's order, with base `{ threadId, runId: run.id, nodeId: <request node>, providerInstanceId: run.providerInstanceId }`: (1) `runtime-request.updated` `{ id: requestId, nodeId: <request node>, providerTurnId, nativeRequestRef: null, kind: user_input, status: pending, responseCapability: { type: message }, createdAt: now, resolvedAt: null }`; (2) `node.updated` `{ id: idAllocator.derive.approvalNode({ requestId }), kind: user_input_request, status: waiting, parentNodeId = rootNodeId = run.rootNodeId, countsForRun: false, providerThreadId: run.providerThreadId, providerTurnId, runtimeRequestId: requestId, … }`; (3) `turn-item.updated` `{ id: idAllocator.derive.approvalTurnItem({ requestId }), nodeId: <request node>, runId, ordinal: projectionStore.getNextTurnItemOrdinal, status: waiting, title: null, type: user_input_request, requestId, questions, responseMode: message }` (the Codex message-mode item). Ids are upstream's own request derivations: `node:runtime-request:loom-ask%3A<id>` / `turn-item:runtime-request:loom-ask%3A<id>` (globally unique because the request id is). `providerTurnId` is upstream's non-exported `providerTurnForRun` rule, inlined (the active attempt's latest turn, else the attempt's `providerTurnId`, else null). **For 3a-4:** `message` capability exempts the request from upstream's turn-terminal dismissal (`ProviderEventIngestor.dismissNativeUserInputs`, `EventSink.guardUserInputCancellations`), so it outlives the provider turn — the respond hunk, supersede rule 6, or a run-end path must close it                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | upstream's mechanism is the baseline (strategy §4.3); The decider only _withholds_; delivery is post-commit: the ask reactor (`loom/userInput/askUserQuestion.ts`) settles the waiter from every committed non-pending `loom-ask:` `runtime-request.updated` (answered, dismissed, closed), so a rolled-back respond never reaches pi. Live = unsettled and a poll attached now or detached < 3 s ago (the extension re-polls at once; past that, upstream's message delivery stands). The registry (`loom/userInput/askWaiters.ts`) is a `Context.Reference` whose default IS the process registry — Effect caches a reference default per key — so the respond hunk, the routes and the reactor share it with no layer and hand-built orchestrator layers need no provide; a test provides a fresh one. Ids: request `loom-ask:<enc threadId>:<enc pi toolCallId>`, command `stableCommandId(caller, toolCallId, "ask_user_question")` (DL-303's shape; a retried POST is the receipted no-op and reuses the waiter); question ids `q1…qn`. V2's question has no `recommended` slot: the pick is labelled `<label> (Recommended)` with `value` = the plain label, so the answer stays the plain label. Upstream's dismiss resolves the request `resolved` + `decision: cancel` (only node and item are `cancelled`), so outcomes render from `decision`. Outcome texts are V1's `userInputOutcome.ts` wording                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | the decider stays pure and transactional; one prefix comparison plus a map read is the whole hunk (P3-21); a direct hand-off from the decider would deliver before commit                                                                                                                                                                                | brief 3a-4; plan P3-21, P3-26; DL-303, DL-331–333                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | local                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |

| DL-366 | 3b-2 — the wake rails as built (`dispatcher/rails.ts`)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | how do terminal deltas, attention, yield, brief-needed, deadlock, digest and notify map onto the tier table, and what did the composers need?                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | Every rail dispatches through `controlMessage` (Loom commands — `thread.work.submit` quiescent, `thread.attention.raise`, `thread.peer-message.mark-delivered` / `expire` — through `ctx.dispatch` directly). **Steered** (`queue_after_active`, origin `control_notice`): yield (`server:workstream-yield:<childId>:<lastOutcome.eventId>`, payload `yield` + `synthesised` when the submit was the rail's, notification), attention (`server:workstream-attention:<childId>:<reason>:<attentionEpisodes[reason]>`, notice `attention`, reasons exactly `needs_guidance` / `awaiting_acceptance` / `error`), brief-needed and deadlock (3b-1 ids, notices `brief-needed` / `deadlock`). **FYI** (`start_if_idle`, notification): the digest (`server:workstream-digest:<parentId>:<hash of item keys>`). A parent's withheld digest items **piggyback onto any steered wake to it in the same pass** (yield, attention, brief-needed, deadlock — V1 piggybacked on the first two only) and are settled only when that message lands. Terminal item = `outcome !== null` and `lastOutcome.eventId !== null`, held back while `isMemberOfUnresolvedGate`; a resolver (`lastOutcome.decision` and `lastRoute.kind` both `resolve`) is additionally held until `lastRoute.to` is done, so a resolved gate reports as ONE pair — one `gate-resolved` item (key: the resolve route's `eventId`) plus the coder as a reference-only item whose kind is now **`terminal`** (`pairTargetToPayloadItem`; it was a second `gate-resolved`). Without the pair hold the pass-start snapshot reported the reviewer one pass before the coder's resolve landed. Yield composer: `YieldFlags { synthesised, gateParked }` replaces `YieldSynthesised`; an agent's (non-cap-breach) yield from an unresolved gate member now also says the gate is parked. No rail writes or clears a hold; a parent whose row is finished or archived gets no FYI, brief-needed or deadlock wake. **Yield and attention deliberately do not check the parent's state** (gate round 1): they are decision-bearing and the parent is the only actor, so a `done` parent is woken (its child would otherwise sit silent), and an archived or deleted parent rejects the message — a dead episode reported one level further up. V1's runaway wake-rate guard and the "parent already saw it via tool activity" suppression are not ported (no V2 activity log; steered wakes queue rather than storm)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | the brief's id table verbatim; the smoke's one-card step 5 needs the pair to coalesce                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | brief 3b-2; V1 `make` §collectTerminalDeltas…wakeDeadlockedParents                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | local                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| DL-367 | 3b-2 — "already digested" without persistence                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | how does a fresh process know a terminal child, advisory or brief-needed rung was already reported, with no marker rows, contract field or Loom command?                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | **Read back from the parent's stored control messages.** `PassContext.delivered(threadId)` = every `loom.controlPayload` item on the thread's user-role messages, with the message's `createdAt`. A terminal child C (episode time T = `lastOutcome.at`) is told iff an item with `threadId === C` at/after T is a `terminal` / `gate-resolved` digest item (standalone or piggybacked), or the kind-less child item of a `yield` card or `attention` notice (V1's prior-rail suppression); an advisory iff an item with its `threadId` and `kind` exists at/after its episode start; a brief-needed child iff a `brief-needed` notice item names it with `status: rung-<n>` at/after its episode start — which also fixes DL-362's batch hash re-notifying a child already named when a sibling joins or leaves the batch. Proven across a second dispatcher instance where only the derivation (not a receipt on an identical id) can suppress: a later digest carries only the new child. The read is cached per thread while `shell.latestUserMessageAt` is unchanged and dropped on every accepted `message.dispatch` to it, so an idle parent with many finished children costs one message read per change, not per pass. The digest's own receipt is not usable per item (its id hashes the whole set). **Known narrow misses (gate round 1, accepted):** the check keys on message time against `lastOutcome.at`, so (a) a child with `error` attention that submits `done` mid-pass, after the snapshot that queued its attention notice, is never digested as done — the notice landed after its submit; (b) a child that yielded and was then set `done` by a human rather than the parent is not re-reported (V1 suppressed the same case through the yield receipt). Keying on `max(lastOutcome.at, outcomeAt)` would instead re-report every parent-accepted yield, the common case The rail pre-filters the active rows cheaply (`parentThreadId`, `kickoffAt`, `outcome` null, no attention, `activityRunStatus` null, no `pendingRuntimeRequest`), then calls `quiescenceCandidate` with the runs, the latest user-role message and `listChildren(…, { includeArchived: true })`; the run is the latest non-queued one; the report text is that run's last `assistant_message` turn item (a run with none writes a one-line placeholder); `writeSynthesisedReport(threadId, runId, text, formatGrace(ms))` → `<threadId>.quiescent-<runId>.md` with the plan's three-line header, grace rendered "10 minutes" / "1 minute" / "45 seconds" from the grace that applied (human or control); then `thread.work.submit { outcome: "quiescent", reportPath }` under `server:loom:quiescent:<threadId>:<runId>` (receipt-checked first). **Gate exemption (gate round 1 M1):** after the candidate check the rail re-reads the parent's children (`listChildren(…, { includeArchived: true })`) and skips a row whose fresh state has an outcome, attention, or `isWaitingInGate` true — fresh because this pass's re-drive may just have opened the counterpart's round (with the pass-start snapshot a reviewer that looped and ended its turn read as quiet in that pass); a coder in `pendingRework` is not waiting and still yields. A beat is `turn-item.updated`, `provider-turn.updated` or `run-attempt.updated` (taking the event's `occurredAt`) on a thread with a sidecar row. Only a nudge that lands starts the 2-minute nudge grace. **Steer predicate:** the sweep restates the Loom clause of upstream's `dispatchMessage` conversion (`isSteerable`). It requires a `running` run, a `running` provider turn on its active attempt, a provider thread bound to a session, and that session live in `ProviderSessionManagerV2` with `supportsActiveSteering` and `activeSteeringInterruptsTools !== true`. The run's user message must not be a native maintenance command (`/compact`, via upstream's exported `isNativeMaintenanceCommand`). When the predicate fails, `decideStallAction`'s `hasOpenTurn` is false, so the sweep escalates at once and dispatches nothing. It is evaluated only for a fresh episode, so the ladder logic is unchanged. Both branches run on the real orchestrator: a `live` seeded run is steerable (the nudge lands inside the running run, with no queued run), and a non-live one escalates. **Escalation id:** `server:workstream-liveness:stall:<childId>:<episodeMs>`. **Stall context** reads the run's tool and `assistant_message` turn items from the V2 projection, not the pi transcript. The last item wins: a failed tool call or a non-empty assistant message. Ids `server:loom:draft:<drafterId>:{spawn,fork,kickoff}`; the drafter id is a fresh UUID. Guards before anything is created: the source exists, is idle (`activityRunStatus` null, no preparing/starting/running/waiting run — `fork.prepare` would defer on any), and its latest finished run's provider thread is `pi` with a strong `nativeThreadRef` (`fork.prepare` refuses a weak one, which would otherwise leave an orphan drafter root; gate round 1 N3). **Two visible differences from a literal `thread.fork`** (gate round 1 N1, for 3d and Phase 4): the drafter is a ROOT with null lineage (`relationshipToParent: null`, its own `rootThreadId`), not `fork` lineage under the source; and its V2 `forkedFrom` stays null, so upstream's UI shows no fork badge — the source link lives only on the sidecar row's `forkFromThreadId` and on the transfer's `sourceThreadId`. | DL-421 | 3d-1 — reconnect resync (plan A4, the `ws.ts` replay hunk)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | does the client's reconnect re-load the HTTP snapshot, or does `ws.ts` need to replay goals on a resumed `loom: true` subscription?                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | **no `ws.ts` change.** `makeSubscribeInput` reloads the authoritative HTTP snapshot whenever the RPC session is new (`lastAuthoritativeSession !== session`), so every reconnect resyncs goals (and the socket fallback's full snapshot carries them for `loom: true`). Only a foreground resubscribe on the **same** live session resumes with `afterSequence` and no goals; a goal written in the swap between the old stream's interruption and the new subscription attaching to the goal PubSub would be missed until the next new session — accepted (the old subscription stays live while the tab is hidden, so the window is the swap itself). | DL-470 | `loom/workstream/report.ts` — the quiescence rail's synthesised report file (smoke steps 3, 6) | why did no quiet child ever yield on real pi? | **Defect, fixed.** The file name was `<threadId>.quiescent-<runId>.md`; a V2 run id embeds its URI-encoded thread id (`run:thread:<id>:ordinal:<n>`), so for any MCP-spawned child the name was ~265 bytes and `writeFileString` failed `ENAMETOOLONG` (logged `loom.dispatcher.quiescent-report-failed` every pass; C and X never yielded). Now `<threadId>.quiescent-<run id minus the embedded thread id>.md` → `…quiescent-ordinal_2.md`; a run id that does not embed the thread id is kept whole. New `report.test.ts` pins the real smoke ids. The plan's `.quiescent-<runId>.md` wording now means that suffix | every quiet child of every workstream was stuck; the stub-adapter tests use short ids | server.log 19:09:47–19:11:47; `73b9d1c1ab`; smoke steps 3/6 re-run after restart #2 | trivial |

| DL-472 | `loom/orchestration/dispatcher/WorkstreamDispatcher.ts` promotion — the kickoff text (smoke step 2) | why did V's first message carry no gate membership? | **Defect, fixed.** The dispatcher's kickoff sent the raw brief file; `childPrompt.ts` says the dispatcher and `mcp__t3-code__workstream_prompt` deliver an identical first turn, but only the tool used `kickoffText`. So every auto-kicked child lost the role line, the **review-gate line** ("You are inside a review gate…" — the reviewer overlay tells the model to look for it), the completion contract and the eta advice. | DL-473 | `loom/orchestration/liveness/inFlightTool.ts` (smoke step 19) | why did G's 30-minute bash loop read as "possibly spinning" and never as a slow tool? | **Defect, fixed.** The in-flight read matched turn items on the active attempt's `providerTurnId`; real Pi leaves `run-attempt.providerTurnId` null and links the provider turn to the attempt by `runAttemptId`, so no tool was ever in flight on Pi: State C was unsuppressed and State D fired a `spinning` digest after 10 min (live, 08:23Z). Now the attempt's provider turns are read by `runAttemptId`. Live after the fix: Y's FYI digest carried `slow-tool` ("bash in flight ~6 min") at 08:31:51Z and no stall nudge reached G. The testkit sets the attempt's id, which is why 3b's tests passed | a long build or test would have drawn a spurious spinning advisory and no slow-tool line | `db/step18-19.txt`, Y's digests; `c836f9d8ff`; `liveness.test.ts` 11/11 | trivial |
| DL-474 | `orchestration-v2/PullRequestWatchReactor.ts` `endsWithoutRead` (smoke step 14) — **diagnosed, not committed** | after DL-471, why did S's watch end silently on the first sweep? | **Defect, open.** A third subagent refusal: the reactor ends any `subagent`-lineage watch without reading ("a watch left from before those rules ends here"), so a Loom child's watch is stopped at the next 2-minute sweep (S: watch 08:37:21, ended 08:37:21.588 by `server:pr-watch:…`). The fix needs the Loom row in a sweep that reads `ProjectionThreadPullRequests` (no `workstream`): three marked lines (`.artifacts/pull9-p3-smoke/proposed-DL-474-PullRequestWatchReactor.patch`), over the smoke's one-line threshold, so it is **not committed**. The orchestrator decides: adopt the patch (and inventory row) or re-route the exemption | as committed, no Loom shipper can watch its PR (P3-21's "two places" is three) | S's events 08:37–08:56; `db/step14.txt` | small; plan + inventory |
| DL-475 | Quiescence after a control-continued human turn (smoke steps 17, 21) — **diagnosed, design question** | why did D, driven only by the human, yield to the root twice? | `quiescenceCandidate` decides human-started from the latest user message. A human-started turn that is continued by a control message — upstream's restart continuation ("Continue where you left off.", `createdBy agent`), Loom's steer redelivery, or 3c's reroute resume — becomes control-started, so 60 s after it ends the rail synthesises a report and yields D with `awaiting_orchestrator` (08:43:04Z after the step-17 restart; 09:14:44Z after the step-21 reroute). The root (told to leave D alone) did; a different orchestrator would act on a thread the human is driving. Related: the stashed steer was redelivered at 08:35:21.537Z while the continuation run was still `starting`, so it queued and ran as the **next** run (ordinal 5), not inside the continuation (ordinal 4) — delivered once, but the plan's "continuation run contains the steer" wording is not what happens. Not fixed: changing what counts as human-started touches Phase 2's predicate shared with 3a/3d (cf. DL-368) | human-driven children get spurious yields after every restart or reroute | D's runs 3–8; receipts `server:loom:quiescent:…:3:…ordinal:5|7` | decision for the orchestrator |

The proxy refused every request (`400 Third-party apps now draw from extra usage`) until the global `@blackbelt-technology/pi-anthropic-messages` package was **copied** into `pi-agent/extensions/` — that bridge is load-bearing for `cliproxy/*`. 70/70 captured pi spawns carried the isolated `PI_CODING_AGENT_DIR`; no global tool registered; `~/.pi/agent` hashes unchanged. (2) The scratch repos live under the Loom worktree, so pi also loaded Loom's `AGENTS.md` as a context file (the scratch repo's own `AGENTS.md` says to ignore it). (3) Step 20: children share one checkout, so "X's worktree deleted" was simulated by moving the project directory away for the consult (09:04:12–09:06:06Z), asked from the step-22 root in smoke-repo-b. (4) Step 21 is **live pipeline, simulated provider**: a local Anthropic-messages endpoint answering the proxy's recorded "cooling down" 429; pi, adapter, classifier and reroute ran for real. (5) Board states that lasted seconds (steps 1–4) are evidenced by a 10 s DB poll (`db-timeline-ws.log`) plus later screenshots. Cause: with no `resetAt` (DL-477d — live pi hides `Retry-After`) the only exhaustion signal is the in-memory health registry, which a restart empties, so `intendedOut` reads false. Consequence: after any restart or deploy during an exhaustion window, every rerouted pi thread goes back to its exhausted model; the next turn fails `usage_limit` once more and is rerouted again.
